| RQ-C02-507 | capability | v1-core | The compiler MUST reject any capability view that escapes the lifetime of its underlying owner. | 0126 §55. Không cho view escape owner | GAP |
| RQ-C02-508 | capability | v1-core | Passing a view<T> to another execution context MUST be rejected unless the view, its underlying storage, and execution contract satisfy both Send and Share semantics. | 0126 §56. Cross-thread view | GAP |
| RQ-C02-509 | capability | v1-core | The compiler MUST NOT permit parent and child execution contexts to simultaneously hold active mut_view capabilities for the same storage. | 0126 §57. `mut_view` cross-thread | GAP |
| RQ-C02-510 | concurrency | v1-core | A `Send` operation transferring value across execution contexts MUST be a Move that permanently revokes access from the sender and establishes exclusive ownership in the receiver. [needs RQ-C02-179, RQ-C02-182] | 0126 §58. Send là ownership transfer | GAP |
| RQ-C02-511 | concurrency | v1-core | The Share property MUST permit creating concurrent read view<T> capabilities across execution contexts without permitting concurrent mutation. [needs RQ-C02-180] | 0126 §59. Share là concurrent capability | GAP |
| RQ-C02-512 | concurrency | v1-core | Synchronization primitives MUST establish explicit happens-before relationships between execution contexts. [needs RQ-C02-184] | 0126 §60. Happens-before | GAP |
| RQ-C02-513 | compiler | v1-core | The compiler optimizer MAY reorder ordinary memory operations but MUST preserve observable single-thread semantics, atomic orderings, synchronization happens-before relations, volatile semantics, and FFI contracts. [needs RQ-C02-184] | 0126 §61. Compiler optimization | GAP |
| RQ-C02-514 | concurrency | v1-core | The compiler backend MUST preserve explicit atomic fence semantics according to the specified memory ordering. | 0126 §62. Atomic fence | GAP |
| RQ-C02-515 | concurrency | v1-core | The core language compiler MUST NOT attempt implicit ABA mitigation, delegating pointer reuse safety to library or system-layer abstractions. | 0126 §63. ABA | GAP |
| RQ-C02-516 | concurrency | v1-core | Atomic pointer operations MUST NOT be assumed to guarantee safe memory reclamation. | 0126 §64. Memory reclamation | GAP |
| RQ-C02-517 | concurrency | v1-core | An `Atomic<*mut T>` raw pointer MUST preserve raw pointer semantics and MUST NOT be assumed to be safe, owned, valid, or dereferenceable. | 0126 §65. Atomic pointer | GAP |
| RQ-C02-518 | concurrency | v1-core | Lock-free data structures using unsafe atomic techniques MUST encapsulate their invariants within safe abstractions before exposing them to safe code. | 0126 §66. Unsafe lock-free structures | GAP |
| RQ-C02-519 | concurrency | v1-core | Thread cancellation in Axiom Core MUST be cooperative, and the core runtime MUST NOT provide safe asynchronous thread killing. | 0126 §67. Cancellation | GAP |
| RQ-C02-520 | async | v1-core | Dropping a suspended Future MUST release internal Future state and owned values, but MUST NOT imply cancellation of external operations initiated by the Future. [needs RQ-C02-520] | 0126 §68. Future cancellation | GAP |
| RQ-C02-521 | async | v1-core | Dropping a Future MUST deterministically clean up all owned resources and internal state within the Future. | 0126 §69. Cancellation và Drop | GAP |
| RQ-C02-522 | concurrency | v1-core | A panic in a child thread MUST NOT implicitly unwind the parent thread directly, communicating failure through the JoinHandle unless configured for `panic=abort`. | 0126 §70. Panic trong thread | GAP |
| RQ-C02-523 | async | v1-core | An executor MUST capture a panicked async task state and expose it as an explicit task failure representation upon joining or awaiting. | 0126 §71. Panic trong task | GAP |
| RQ-C02-524 | concurrency | v1-core | The runtime and standard library MUST NOT conflate panic semantics with cancellation semantics, treating them as separate operational states. | 0126 §72. Panic ≠ cancellation | GAP |
| RQ-C02-525 | concurrency | v1-core | The core language compiler MUST NOT be required to detect all dynamic deadlocks or enforce static lock acquisition hierarchies in user code. | 0126 §73. Deadlock | GAP |
| RQ-C02-526 | concurrency | v1-core | Safe Axiom code MUST NOT create data races (ACONC-20). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-527 | concurrency | v1-core | The `Send` property MUST permit ownership transfer across execution contexts (ACONC-21). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-528 | concurrency | v1-core | The `Share` property MUST permit safe concurrent read sharing when its contract is satisfied (ACONC-22). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-529 | concurrency | v1-core | The compiler MUST treat `Send` and `Share` as independent properties (ACONC-23). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-530 | concurrency | v1-core | Cross-context ownership transfer MUST be a Move operation (ACONC-24). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-531 | concurrency | v1-core | Concurrent access to values or capabilities MUST require valid synchronization or immutable sharing (ACONC-25). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-532 | concurrency | v1-core | Synchronization operations MUST establish explicit happens-before relations (ACONC-26). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-533 | concurrency | v1-core | Atomic ordering MUST be preserved by compiler optimization passes (ACONC-27). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-534 | concurrency | v1-core | Atomic access MUST NOT be bypassed through ordinary mutable capabilities (ACONC-28). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-535 | concurrency | v1-core | Synchronous lock guards MUST NOT cross suspension points (ACONC-29). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-536 | concurrency | v1-core | Synchronization MUST be defined as a property of types and abstractions (ACONC-30). | 0126 §75. Thread safety invariant | GAP |
| RQ-C02-537 | concurrency | v1-core | The language MUST NOT introduce separate concurrency pointer types such as `ThreadRef` or `SharedRef`, relying on unified ownership and capability semantics. | 0126 §79. Không tạo concurrency pointer type | GAP |
| RQ-C02-538 | concurrency | v1-core | The compiler and runtime MUST NOT define separate ownership systems for threads, async tasks, and GPU invocations, maintaining a single unified ownership model. | 0126 §80. Kiến trúc cuối cùng | GAP |
| RQ-C02-539 | concurrency | v1-core | The language architecture MUST keep distinct the foundational concepts of Send and Share, Move and Copy, Ownership and Capability, Capability and Synchronization, Atomic and Volatile, Thread and Task, Panic and Cancellation, Arc and Mutex, and Mutex and Atomic. | 0126 §81. Freeze Part V | GAP |
| RQ-C02-540 | async | v1-core | An `async fn` must construct an uncompleted computation represented by a Future that yields Pending or Ready(T) upon polling rather than executing synchronously to completion. | 0127 §2. `async fn` | GAP |
| RQ-C02-541 | async | v1-core | The compiler must support storing Futures in registers, the stack, inline structs, caller-provided storage, arenas, or the heap without mandating heap allocation. | 0127 §4. Future không bắt buộc heap | GAP |
| RQ-C02-542 | async | v1-core | The compiler is permitted to lower an async function lacking suspension points into an ordinary synchronous function only when the async abstraction is not observable. | 0127 §5. Future không bắt buộc runtime | GAP |
| RQ-C02-543 | async | v1-core | The compiler must not alter an observable public API type contract even if the implementation requires no suspension, maintaining the async computation contract. | 0127 §6. Nhưng semantic type vẫn phải nhất quán | GAP |
| RQ-C02-544 | async | v1-core | The compiler must drop variables whose live ranges end prior to an `.await` suspension point before suspension and exclude them from the Future state [needs RQ-C02-544]. | 0127 §9. Chỉ lưu state cần thiết | GAP |
| RQ-C02-545 | async | v1-core | The compiler must perform live-across-await analysis combining liveness, ownership, and drop analysis so that only values live across suspension become fields of the Future state [needs RQ-C02-544]. | 0127 §10. Live-across-await analysis | GAP |
| RQ-C02-546 | async | v1-core | Owned values may cross `.await` suspension points via compiler-managed internal ownership transfer into the Future state and back to local ownership upon resumption. | 0127 §12. Owned values qua await | GAP |
| RQ-C02-547 | async | v1-core | The compiler must infer all Future and async lifetime dependencies automatically without requiring explicit lifetime syntax on Futures or async functions. | 0127 §13. Không có lifetime syntax | GAP |
| RQ-C02-548 | async | v1-core | The compiler must reject any program where a `view<T>` capability crosses an `.await` suspension boundary in v0.1. | 0127 §14. `view<T>` qua await | GAP |
| RQ-C02-549 | async | v1-core | The compiler must reject any program where a `mut_view<T>` capability crosses an `.await` suspension boundary in v0.1. | 0127 §15. `mut_view<T>` qua await | GAP |
| RQ-C02-550 | async | v1-core | Capabilities including `view<T>` and `mut_view<T>` may be re-borrowed after an `.await` boundary, but their lifetimes are strictly confined to the immediate synchronous execution region. | 0127 §17. Có thể tạo view lại sau await | GAP |
| RQ-C02-551 | async | v1-core | The compiler must prevent Future state machines from persisting mutable capabilities referencing state-owned data across suspension points. | 0127 §18. `mut_view` và Future state | GAP |
| RQ-C02-552 | async | v1-core | The `.await` expression must act as a semantic suspension boundary where drop, ownership, capability expiration, panic, cancellation, and Send constraints are validated before state machine lowering. | 0127 §19. Await là suspension boundary | GAP |
| RQ-C02-553 | async | v1-core | Local resources whose lexical scope closes before an `.await` point must be dropped before suspension rather than held until function exit. | 0127 §20. Cleanup trước suspension | GAP |
| RQ-C02-554 | async | v1-core | Dropping a Future while Pending must deterministically drop all owned values held within the Future state without triggering a panic or terminating the OS thread. | 0127 §21. Cancellation | GAP |
| RQ-C02-555 | async | v1-core | Dropping a Future does not imply automatic cancellation of external OS or network operations, requiring explicit cooperative cancellation mechanisms. | 0127 §23. External operation | GAP |
| RQ-C02-556 | async | v1-core | The language core must not require a specific executor or large runtime, allowing async execution in freestanding and embedded environments. | 0127 §25. Executor | GAP |
| RQ-C02-557 | async | v1-core | Future wake notifications must operate through library or runtime Waker abstractions without implicitly spawning OS threads. | 0127 §27. Waker | GAP |
| RQ-C02-558 | async | v1-core | The `.await` expression must not spawn a new task or execution context, performing only in-place polling and suspension. | 0127 §29. `await` không spawn | GAP |

| RQ-C02-560 | async | v1-core | A Future passed to `spawn` in a multi-threaded executor must satisfy the `Send` contract together with all values live across its suspension points [needs RQ-C02-560]. | 0127 §32. `spawn` | GAP |

| RQ-C02-563 | async | v1-core | Polling a Future requires exclusive access (`&mut Future`), and concurrent polling of the same Future across threads without synchronization is prohibited as Futures do not implicitly implement `Share`. | 0127 §37. Poll độc quyền | GAP |

| RQ-C02-572 | async | v1-core | Upon Future completion or cancellation, all remaining owned fields within the Future state must be dropped in deterministic order according to standard ownership rules. | 0127 §50. Drop order trong Future | GAP |

| RQ-C02-574 | async | v1-core | Holding a lock across an `.await` boundary requires an async-aware synchronization primitive and must not use synchronous blocking primitives. | 0127 §52. Async mutex | GAP |

| RQ-C02-579 | async | v1-core | The semantic test oracle must support observable async lifecycle trace events including SUSPEND, RESUME, POLL, WAKE, TASK_CREATE, and TASK_DROP [needs RQ-C02-579]. | 0127 §57. Oracle | GAP |

| RQ-C02-605 | memory-model | v1-core | The memory model must distinguish between ordinary memory access and atomic memory access, prohibiting arbitrary mixing of ordinary and atomic access modes on the same storage. | 0128 §1 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-606 | atomics | v1-core | Atomicity must be a property of the underlying storage rather than of ordinary values subjected to atomic operations (AATOM-20). | 0128 §2, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-607 | atomics | v1-core | Atomic storage must not be accessed through ordinary mutable capabilities such as mut_view to bypass atomic semantics (AATOM-21). | 0128 §3, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-608 | atomics | v1-core | An atomic load operation must return an ordinary non-atomic value upon completion. | 0128 §5 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-609 | atomics | v1-core | An atomic store operation must write a value atomically and establish release ordering constraints for preceding ordinary memory operations when specified. | 0128 §6 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-610 | atomics | v1-core | Relaxed atomic operations must provide atomicity and object modification order without establishing synchronization with ordinary memory (AATOM-22). | 0128 §7, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-611 | atomics | v1-core | Acquire atomic operations must establish acquire ordering such that prior writes in a releasing thread are ordered before subsequent reads in the acquiring thread (AATOM-23). | 0128 §8, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-612 | atomics | v1-core | Release atomic operations must establish release ordering such that preceding writes are published and ordered before the release operation becomes visible (AATOM-24). | 0128 §9, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-613 | atomics | v1-core | The compiler must not automatically upgrade Relaxed atomic operations to Acquire or Release ordering. | 0128 §10 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-614 | atomics | v1-core | AcqRel atomic operations must combine acquire and release semantics on read-modify-write operations to simultaneously synchronize with prior writes and publish subsequent modifications (AATOM-25). | 0128 §11, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-615 | atomics | v1-core | Sequentially consistent (SeqCst) atomic operations must provide the strongest atomic ordering and must not be weakened to Acquire, Release, or Relaxed by compiler optimizations (AATOM-26). | 0128 §12, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-616 | atomics | v1-core | Each atomic object must maintain a single, consistent total modification order across all atomic modifications performed upon it. | 0128 §13 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-617 | atomics | v1-core | Atomic operations must be indivisible without partial atomic reads or writes, requiring runtime emulation if unsupported natively by the target architecture. | 0128 §14 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-618 | atomics | v1-core | Atomic operations must not be assumed to be lock-free by default (AATOM-29). | 0128 §15, §16, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-619 | atomics | v1-core | Atomic operations must not be assumed to be wait-free by default (AATOM-30). | 0128 §15, §17, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-620 | atomics | v1-full | The standard library or compiler must provide a target-aware mechanism to query whether an atomic type or operation is lock-free on the target platform. | 0128 §18 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-621 | atomics | v1-core | The language and runtime must support both strong compare-and-exchange and weak compare-and-exchange operations. | 0128 §19 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-622 | atomics | v1-core | Strong compare-and-exchange (compare_exchange) must not fail spuriously when the current value matches the expected value. | 0128 §20 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-623 | atomics | v1-core | Weak compare-and-exchange (compare_exchange_weak) is permitted to fail spuriously even when the current value matches the expected value. | 0128 §21 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-624 | atomics | v1-core | The compiler must validate that failure memory ordering in compare-and-exchange operations does not specify Release or AcqRel semantics. | 0128 §22 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-625 | atomics | v1-core | Primitive atomic read-modify-write operations (fetch_add, fetch_sub, fetch_and, fetch_or, fetch_xor, swap) must execute as a single atomic modification in the object's modification order. | 0128 §23 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-626 | atomics | v1-core | Atomic read-modify-write integer operations must use wrapping two's-complement arithmetic based on the bit-width of the atomic integer type (AATOM-39). | 0128 §25, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-627 | atomics | v1-core | The memory model must support explicit memory fences parameterized by Acquire, Release, AcqRel, and SeqCst orderings. | 0128 §26 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-628 | atomics | v1-core | Memory fences must establish execution ordering constraints without reading from or writing to memory. | 0128 §27, §28 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-629 | compiler | v1-full | The system layer may provide a compiler-only barrier (compiler_fence) that restricts instruction reordering by the compiler without emitting hardware atomic synchronization instructions. | 0128 §29 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-630 | memory-model | v1-core | Volatile memory accesses must guarantee the execution of external observable memory effects without establishing thread synchronization or happens-before relations (AATOM-28). | 0128 §30, §31, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-631 | memory-model | v1-full | The system layer must provide volatile load and store operations for accessing external and device memory-mapped I/O registers. | 0128 §32 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-632 | memory-model | v1-core | Compiler optimizations on safe ordinary memory must preserve capability rules, ownership rules, and observable program semantics. | 0128 §33 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-633 | atomics | v1-core | Compiler optimizations must not weaken atomic ordering or eliminate atomic operations unless it is formally proven that complete atomic memory model semantics are preserved (AATOM-27, AATOM-40). | 0128 §34, §35, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-634 | memory-model | v1-core | The memory model must define a formal happens-before relation established by sequenced-before order and synchronizes-with contracts (AATOM-35). | 0128 §36, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-635 | memory-model | v1-core | Within a single thread context, compiler instruction reordering must preserve sequenced-before evaluation order and observable execution results. | 0128 §37 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-636 | atomics | v1-core | An Acquire atomic read that observes a Release atomic write must establish a synchronizes-with relationship that orders prior writes before subsequent reads. | 0128 §38 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-637 | safety | v1-core | Safe ordinary memory must not participate in a data race, and concurrent access combining ordinary and atomic access to the same storage must be prohibited (AATOM-34). | 0128 §39, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-638 | atomics | v1-core | Atomic synchronization semantics must bind to distinct storage identities rather than type identities alone. | 0128 §40 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-639 | memory-layout | v1-full | The standard library must provide target-aware alignment constants to allow atomic storage structures to prevent false sharing. | 0128 §41, §42 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-640 | memory-layout | post-v1 | A @cache_align attribute for automatic target-specific cache-line padding shall be deferred as a post-v1 system feature. | 0128 §43 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-641 | atomics | v1-full | Atomic pointer operations (Atomic<*mut T>) must only ensure atomicity of the pointer address manipulation without guaranteeing or extending the referenced object's lifetime, validity, or ownership (AATOM-31). | 0128 §44, §45, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-642 | atomics | v1-full | Lock-free structures built on raw pointers must be encapsulated in safe abstractions where lifetime and memory reclamation safety are proven [needs RQ-C02-642]. | 0128 §46 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-643 | atomics | v1-core | Primitive atomic operations must not be expected to detect or prevent the ABA problem (AATOM-32). | 0128 §47, §48, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-644 | memory-management | v1-full | Safe memory reclamation mechanisms for lock-free pointer structures must be implemented at the library or system layer and kept distinct from primitive atomic synchronization (AATOM-33). | 0128 §50, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-645 | memory-management | v1-core | Reference counting must never be implicitly inserted for atomic pointers or references and must only exist when explicitly chosen via library abstractions. | 0128 §51 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-646 | concurrency | v1-full | The Arc<T> library abstraction must not imply that access to the inner payload is atomic, and concurrent mutation through Arc<T> must require explicit synchronization primitives [needs RQ-C02-392]. | 0128 §52, §53, §54 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-647 | memory-management | v1-core | In concurrent and lock-free structures, logical removal of a resource must remain distinct from physical memory deallocation and Drop execution (AMEM-43). | 0128 §55 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-648 | safety | v1-core | Safe code errors including data races, out-of-bounds accesses, and use-after-free must be rejected statically or trigger defined runtime panic or trap behaviors, never undefined behavior exploited for optimization. | 0128 §57 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-649 | safety | v1-core | Code enclosed in unsafe blocks must not permit the compiler to weaken atomic memory ordering or violate atomic synchronization semantics (AATOM-36). | 0128 §58, §59, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-650 | ffi | v1-core | FFI boundaries must be treated conservatively by default, requiring an explicit contract to establish atomic synchronization or happens-before relationships across foreign functions (AATOM-37). | 0128 §60, §61, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-651 | ffi | v1-full | Hardware and MMIO registers accessed across FFI must be mediated through explicit volatile operations rather than ordinary memory accesses. | 0128 §62 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-652 | gpu | post-v1 | GPU atomic operations must extend the atomic memory model with hierarchical memory scopes without replacing or altering core atomic ordering semantics (AATOM-38) [needs RQ-C02-652]. | 0128 §63, §64, §66 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-653 | layout | v1-core | The compiler shall be permitted to choose an optimized memory layout for data types unless an explicit ABI representation attribute is specified. | 0129 §1 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-732 | expressions | v1-core | If an evaluation order can affect the program result, Axiom MUST explicitly specify that order. | 0130 §0 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-733 | expressions | v1-core | The compiler MAY freely optimize instruction scheduling after semantic results have been determined, provided observable semantics remain unchanged. | 0130 §0 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-734 | expressions | v1-core | Every expression in Axiom MUST be classified internally by the compiler into one or more semantic categories: VALUE, PLACE, CAPABILITY, or NEVER. | 0130 §1 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-735 | expressions | v1-core | The compiler MUST track the provenance and region of every capability expression. | 0130 §1 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-736 | expressions | v1-core | An expression of type never MAY coerce to the expected context expression type without creating a runtime conversion. | 0130 §1 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-738 | expressions | v1-core | The compiler MUST NOT reorder subexpression evaluation if doing so alters observable program semantics. [needs RQ-C02-738] | 0130 §2 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-739 | expressions | v1-core | Method call evaluation receiver.method(arg1, arg2) MUST strictly follow the order: evaluate receiver, resolve method, evaluate argument expressions left-to-right, and perform the call. | 0130 §3 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-740 | expressions | v1-core | Method call syntax MUST preserve the evaluation order of the receiver before arguments as syntactic sugar for function calls. | 0130 §3 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-741 | expressions | v1-core | Each function argument expression MUST be evaluated exactly once. [needs RQ-C02-741] | 0130 §4 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-742 | expressions | v1-core | Logical AND (&&) and logical OR (\|\|) MUST operate as control-flow operators with short-circuit evaluation rather than overloadable function operators. [needs RQ-C02-743] | 0130 §5 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-743 | expressions | v1-core | In a logical AND expression (a && b), the right-hand operand MUST NOT be evaluated if the left-hand operand evaluates to false. [needs RQ-C02-743] | 0130 §5 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-744 | expressions | v1-core | In a logical OR expression (a \|\| b), the right-hand operand MUST NOT be evaluated if the left-hand operand evaluates to true. [needs RQ-C02-743] | 0130 §5 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-745 | expressions | v1-core | The compiler MUST NOT perform eager evaluation on guarded or short-circuited expressions. | 0130 §6 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-746 | expressions | v1-core | Expressions MUST adhere to the language operator precedence hierarchy, evaluating primary expressions highest and assignments lowest. | 0130 §7 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-747 | expressions | v1-core | Operators ++, --, and ?: MUST NOT be included in Axiom v0.1. | 0130 §7 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-748 | expressions | v1-core | Parentheses MUST always be permitted to override or clarify operator precedence. | 0130 §7 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-750 | expressions | v1-core | Arithmetic operands with mismatched types MUST result in a compile error unless explicitly converted. [needs RQ-C02-749] | 0130 §8 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-751 | expressions | v1-core | Safe checked arithmetic operations (+, -, *) MUST panic or trap upon overflow. | 0130 §9 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-752 | expressions | v1-core | Wrapping arithmetic operators (+%, -%, *%) MUST compute modulo the bit-width of the operand type. | 0130 §9 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-754 | expressions | v1-core | Integer division by zero (a / 0) MUST panic or trap. | 0130 §10 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-755 | expressions | v1-core | Division of the minimum signed integer value by -1 (MIN_INT / -1) MUST panic or trap in checked mode without undefined behavior. | 0130 §10 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-757 | expressions | v1-core | Equality comparison (==) MUST resolve to semantic equality via traits or intrinsics rather than raw memory byte comparison (memcmp). | 0130 §12 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-758 | expressions | v1-core | Padding bytes, internal pointer representations, and layout optimizations MUST NOT affect the result of semantic equality. | 0130 §12 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-759 | expressions | v1-core | Operator overloading MUST be resolved through exact deterministic trait matching without implicit conversion ranking. [needs RQ-C02-759] | 0130 §13 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-762 | expressions | v1-core | Indexing expressions a[i] MUST evaluate the container a before evaluating the index expression i. | 0130 §15 | GAP |
| RQ-C02-763 | expressions | v1-core | Safe indexing expressions MUST perform bounds and provenance verification before accessing the element. [needs RQ-C02-763] | 0130 §15 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-868 | functions | v1-core | Functions shall be high-level behavior abstractions that the compiler can lower directly into machine code without runtime machinery when none is required. | 0131 - Goal | GAP |

| RQ-C02-2216 | ownership | v1-core | Dataflow analysis states (MAYBE_*) MUST NOT exist as language-level states. | 0160 §2 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-2220 | ownership | v1-core | The Copy property MUST be treated as a semantic property rather than bitwise memory copyability. | 0160 §6 | GAP |

| RQ-C02-2224 | ownership | v1-core | Assigning to a previously moved variable MUST transition its place to OWNED and restore it for subsequent use. | 0160 §10 | GAP |
